<div xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" id="book-columns"><div xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" id="book-inner">
<p id="auto_bookmark_toc_18" class="P__STAR__STAR__STAR__page_break"><span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.1.1">Chapter 7:</span> <span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.2.1">Quadrilateral of Offense and Defense</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.3.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 2.40em; letter-spacing: 0.60mm;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.4.1">I</span></span><span style=" font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.5.1">T SEEMED LIKE</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.6.1"> the teachers had taken care of preparations for the special exam the day before, because when I had arrived at school at the usual time in the morning, I noticed that the classroom was arranged differently than usual. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.7.1">The five desks in the frontmost row where Horikita and some other students sat had been pushed slightly forward, and each of those desks had a tablet and accompanying stylus pen placed on them. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.7.2">Additionally, each desk was sandwiched by partitions to prevent cheating. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.7.3">I’m sure that the tablets were equipped with privacy screen filters in addition to the partitions. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.7.4">The role of the partitions may have been to prevent indirect forms of communication, such as eye contact, for example. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.7.5">Five new desks and chairs were placed in the back behind the last row of desks.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.8.1">A maximum of five students—the ones who would be nominated—would sit in the frontmost row and solve problems. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.8.2">With the teacher monitoring them closely, it would be difficult for them to cheat.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.9.1">“Did you sleep last night?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.9.2">I asked Horikita, who had just taken a seat behind me.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.10.1">“I slept like normal. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.10.2">There was nothing more I could do anyway, and all that was left was for me to take proper care of my health,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.11.1">“You had a fever during our first uninhabited island exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.11.2">That was tough,” I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.12.1">“Shut up. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.12.2">I’ll stab you,” snapped Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.13.1">“Sorry,” I didn’t understand exactly why she was going to stab me, but I didn’t like being stabbed regardless. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.13.2">I figured I’d just apologize.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.14.1">“Feel calm?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.14.2">she asked.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.15.1">“Not at all. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.15.2">If anything, it may affect my performance, so I apologize in advance,” I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.16.1">At the very least, I was certain that neither Sakayanagi nor Ichinose would hit me with a frontal academic attack.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.17.1">“Sorry, but I will absolutely not grant you protection,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.18.1">“Never, huh?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.18.2">I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.19.1">Wow, what a position to be in, to be told from the very beginning that I could not receive any protection. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.19.2">She seemed to be half joking, but I thought it best to take her words at face value. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.19.3">Shortly afterward, after confirming that there were no absences and thus all the students in Horikita’s class were present, Chabashira-sensei wished us good luck and left the classroom.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.20.1">It was becoming customary for these exams to be monitored by a homeroom teacher from another class rather than by their own homeroom teacher as a necessary measure to prevent unfairness. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.20.2">Sakagami-sensei, the homeroom instructor for Ryuuen’s class, appeared in the classroom.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.21.1">“My name is Sakagami, and I’ll be overseeing your class this time. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.21.2">I will now provide you with information about today’s special exam, the order of attack, and other things of note,” explained Sakagami-sensei.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.22.1">After giving that short statement in a calm tone, Sakagami-sensei closed his mouth and kept it shut for a long while afterward. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.22.2">He operated the tablet wordlessly, displaying the placement and notes up on the monitor.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.23.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.24.1">SPECIAL EXAM – CONFIGURATION</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.25.1">1: Class A → 2: Class B</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.26.1">  ↑     ↓</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.27.1">3: Class D ← 3: Class C</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.28.1">NOTES</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.29.1">Trips to the bathroom can only be made during the 10-minute break window held every four turns.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.30.1">After 10 turns (the first half), there will be a 40-minute break for lunch.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.31.1">Conversation among yourselves and cell phone use are permitted except when nominated students are solving problems.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.32.1">Students deemed unable to continue the examination due poor physical health or other circumstances will be treated as an elimination.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.33.1">Any student found to be cheating will be immediately eliminated and the points that student has earned will be forfeited.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.34.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.1">New information was included in the notes section, but there wasn’t anything particularly surprising. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.2">Nominated students couldn’t do anything to escape from an attack by faking sick, nor would doing anything that might cause delays be allowed. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.3">Unlike a normal written exam, the questions would be different for each participant, so students couldn’t go hide in the bathroom and give each other the answers. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.4">Even if you did run into a student from the other class while in the bathroom, it wouldn’t be a problem. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.5">It was a pretty lukewarm restriction, and it didn’t matter when you could use your cell phone anyway. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.35.6">The class configuration and the order of attack displayed on the monitor was more important.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.1">First, the turn would begin with Class B—or rather, Horikita’s class—who would attack Ichinose’s Class C. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.2">Next, Ichinose would attack Ryuuen’s Class D, and then Ryuuen would attack Sakayanagi’s Class A. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.3">Finally, Sakayanagi’s Class A would attack Horikita’s class, completing one loop. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.4">That would repeat for a total of ten turns. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.5">Then, when we entered the second half of the exam, that order would be reversed, moving counterclockwise. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.6">Horikita stared at the monitor to check the flow of attacking and defending, and then immediately directed her attention to her phone. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.36.7">All of Horikita’s strategies against Ryuuen’s class had become unnecessary.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.37.1">She must have moved on to pulling the offensive and defensive plans against Ichinose’s and Sakayanagi’s classes out of her head. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.37.2">If we were to evaluate the situation only on a superficial level, having Ichinose’s class as one of our opponents was a positive, as they were likely to only take a straightforward approach. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.37.3">On the other hand, facing off against Sakayanagi’s class was a negative. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.37.4">In addition to her class’s overall strength, she had sharp insight and would work on fraying our nerves.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.38.1">I figured that I would sit back and watch to see how it’d play out. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.38.2">Not that I could actually admit to that level of detachment.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.1">I thought back to the sixteen categories featured in this special exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.2">I didn’t intend to write off “elimination = expulsion,” but even looking back at the past two years of special exams, it was an unusually rare rule. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.3">Someone like me normally wasn’t really at much of a risk. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.4">I could make it through the academic category, but I was below average in areas like subcultures and performing arts. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.5">Even though we could make three exemptions in advance and protect ourselves, there was still a reasonable chance of encountering a problem that I didn’t know the answer to. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.39.6">I couldn’t rule out the possibility that I could be eliminated.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.40.1">If Sakayanagi and Ichinose had come to some arrangement before the exam and decided that they’d make me the only elimination and we came in last place, then expulsion would inevitably await. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.40.2">As a student, I didn’t think of this as an unreasonable rule—actually, this exam would be the only way some people would shine. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.40.3">It was a good opportunity for new talent to be recognized.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.41.1">“Now, we will begin the special exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.41.2">On the first turn, this class, the first attackers, will be asked to choose a category and difficulty level and nominate five students,” explained Sakagami-sensei.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.1">Sakagami-sensei gave the signal to begin the special exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.2">The time limit for one attack and defense was a mere three minutes. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.3">That didn’t give us any leeway. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.4">We had no time to think, just to communicate what we’d planned. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.5">Since we would be spending far longer observing the movements of the other classes, the better course of action would be to hold discussions during those times. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.42.6">The only time we’d hesitate would be when we hadn’t planned for something.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.43.1">“We’re going in blind at the start. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.43.2">We will proceed as we’ve discussed,” Horikita said firmly as she reached for the tablet that would be the main device. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.43.3">I hadn’t participated in that discussion, so I didn’t have any information on what plan Horikita was going to use. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.43.4">We had to verbally inform the teacher of the category, difficulty level, and nominated students. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.43.5">Sakagami immediately displayed Horikita’s choices up on the monitor.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.44.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.45.1">Category: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.46.1">English </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.47.1">Difficulty Level: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.48.1">1</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.49.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.50.1">Kobashi Yume, Watanabe Norihito, Sumida Makoto, Ninomiya Yui, Shibata Sou</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.51.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.52.1">The category was English, and Horikita’s target were students in Ichinose’s class who were not academically gifted. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.52.2">It was a solid choice as a first move, one that reflected the core of her strategy—an attack made in a category that we could measure objectively. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.52.3">Since we had zero points, we didn’t have anything to spend to change the difficulty. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.52.4">Even in light of the fact that we were the first class to take a turn, choosing an academic subject was a solid move. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.52.5">Horikita’s class, as well as the other classes, would use their first question as a stepping stone to get a grasp on question trends, the standard difficulty level, and so on.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.53.1">However, there were many students in Ichinose’s class that had exceptionally balanced academic abilities. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.53.2">All of the students enrolled in her class currently had an Academic Ability score of C– or higher. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.53.3">Inevitably, the question of which students to nominate would be important but knowing who struggled in what subjects could only be determined from past inquiry or through individual interactions. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.54.1">Aside from their academic standing, though, it would be easier to exploit weaknesses in opponents through irregular categories. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.54.2">Those areas could be deadly for students who were weak in areas like subcultures or performing arts. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.54.3">You’d need courage to attack with that on the first move. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.54.4">Those categories were so irregular that it was more difficult to determine strengths or weaknesses compared to academic categories and harder to figure out the degree of difficulty.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.55.1">Now then, who would Ichinose protect? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.55.2">I found myself looking forward to finding out. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.55.3">After the defending side’s nominations were over, the screen updated.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.56.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.57.1">Successful protections by the defender:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.58.1"> Ninomiya Yui, Watanabe Norihito</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.59.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.60.1">“That means that those two people were protected, then?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.60.2">asked Nishimura, as she looked at the monitor. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.61.1">“…Yes, that’s what it means. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.61.2">Ichinose-san’s class received two points then, unconditionally. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.61.3">Whether we will earn points or not hangs on whether the remaining three students get the question right,” explained Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.62.1">There were three students in Ichinose’s class who had excluded English. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.62.2">Horikita had the right to choose from thirty-six students to nominate, excluding the leader, but the fact that two of the students chosen had been defended because of the protection mechanic wasn’t a happy probability. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.62.3">It was understandable though—Horikita had targeted students who weren’t good at English. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.62.4">Perhaps it was because it was the first turn, but this was a very straightforward gambit. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.62.5">The question that we had assigned to the opposing class was also displayed on the screen for us.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.63.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.64.1">“Rearrange the English words shown in the brackets and add one additional word, so that the English sentence matches the meaning of the Japanese sentence.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.65.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.66.1">誰も成長するためには、ある程度の苦労が常に必要だ。</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.67.1">[everyone/amount/necessary/always/a/grow/of/hardship/for/is/to]</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.68.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.69.1">“Wh-what the heck? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.69.2">Isn’t that, like, really hard?!” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.69.3">wailed Ike, jumping to his feet.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.70.1">The students who were capable of studying, like Horikita and Yousuke, looked at each other with conflicted expressions on their faces.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.71.1">“The questions are pretty difficult,” said Yousuke.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.72.1">“Only if you don’t study,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.73.1">Opinion was split in the class on how difficult the question was. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.73.2">Ichinose’s class had an abundance of students who, though not perfect, had a stable level of academic ability. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.73.3">How well would the lower-ranked members of her class perform? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.73.4">The results of the three remaining students from Ichinose’s class who had been tasked with the first question were shown up on the monitor.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.74.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.75.1">Students who answered correctly:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.76.1"> Kobashi Yume, Shibata Sou</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.77.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.1">Together with the points they gained from the two protections earlier, that brought them to a total of four points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.2">A great start. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.3">Next, Ichinose’s class attacked Ryuuen’s class. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.4">Their chosen category was Economics. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.5">Ryuuen succeeded in defending one person; however, nobody else answered correctly, so they had only scored one point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.6">The class’s academic weaknesses were starting to show. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.7">I’m sure that was painful for them, but there was no time to be discouraged. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.78.8">We’d get a chance to cancel out Ichinose’s lead by scoring four or more points when it was our turn.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.79.1">In this special exam, it was important to not let your opponent get protections or get answers correct, but it was also crucial that you get a high percentage of correct answers while on defense—you only scored while defending. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.79.2">Sakayanagi had protected one person from Ryuuen’s class’s attack, and three of her people had answered the question correctly, scoring four points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.79.3">We’d come to the latter half of the first turn. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.79.4">Sakayanagi’s class was about to begin its attack on Horikita’s class.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.80.1">“It’s finally time,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.81.1">“Yes. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.81.2">I wonder how Sakayanagi-san will attack…” said Yousuke.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.82.1">The category that Sakayanagi had selected was announced.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.83.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.84.1">Category: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.85.1">Mental Calculation</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.86.1">Difficulty Level:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.87.1"> 1</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.88.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.89.1">I assumed that the Mental Calculation category would include some problems that were simply adding or multiplying in our heads, filling in the correct figure to get the correct answer, and so on. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.89.2">How challenging would these problems be at difficulty level 1? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.89.3">Surprisingly, there were many students who struggled with calculations in Horikita’s class, so much so that seven people had chosen it as a category to exclude. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.89.4">The issue of handling Kouenji was of bigger concern to me. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.89.5">If we were to assume that keeping the promise Horikita made to him was a prerequisite, then that would mean she had to protect him.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.90.1">Kouenji had demonstrated outstanding talent in the Uninhabited Island Special Exam, but essentially, he was a free spirit who did whatever he wished and couldn’t be counted on to prep for this. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.90.2">However, there was little reason for the other classes to specifically go out of their way to concentrate on someone like Kouenji, who had a high academic ability and a keen intuition. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.90.3">Still, a promise was a promise. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.90.4">With that in mind, what decision would Horikita make?</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.91.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.92.1">Students nominated for protection by the defender:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.93.1"> Sonoda Chiyo, Ichihashi Ruri, Okiya Kyousuke, Ike Kanji, Makita Susumu</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.94.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.95.1">Kouenji’s name was not among the five students nominated by Horikita for protection. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.95.2">Kouenji, who ignored things like special exams, didn’t even react. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.96.1">“H-hey, Suzune. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.96.2">Are you sure about this? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.96.3">Not protectin’ him?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.96.4">Sudou cried out in panic. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.96.5">It seemed like he had been worrying about Kouenji all along.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.97.1">“This is a special exam in which you only run the risk of expulsion once you have been eliminated. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.97.2">I decided not to protect him until he got two questions wrong. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.97.3">No need to protect him earlier than that.” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.97.4">said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.98.1">“Well, yeah, okay, that makes sense…” replied Sudou. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.98.2">Although he was shocked for a moment, he quickly accepted Horikita’s judgment.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.99.1">“Now, I am simply stating an alternative, but: Kouenji-kun is free to answer the questions seriously or leave them blank. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.99.2">I trust that’s all right with you?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.99.3">said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.100.1">The way Horikita said it made it sound like it was essentially an after-the-fact agreement, but Kouenji didn’t seem bothered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.101.1">“Do whatever you like,” he replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.102.1">Even though she’d promised that she wouldn’t let him be expelled, she was saying that she couldn’t protect him constantly as if he were a baby. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.102.2">You could say that Horikita’s handling of the situation was a conservative one. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.102.3">Moreover, though he was given assurance that he was free to solve the problems however he wished, Kouenji was at liberty to take an active role in protecting himself. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.102.4">There was always that 1 percent chance of uncertainty, even if he was 99 percent safe. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.102.5">Most people wouldn’t put a noose around their own neck.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.103.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.104.1">Successful protections by the defender: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.105.1">Okiya Kyousuke, Ike Kanji</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.106.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.107.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.108.1">Ishikura Kayoko, Kikuchi Eita, Inokashira Kokoro</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.109.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.1">Horikita had brilliantly managed to successfully protect two people in her first defense, scoring us two big points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.2">Just doing that had put us in third place at that moment. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.3">If all three students about to tackle the upcoming question answered correctly, Horikita would tentatively be in first place, but who could say? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.4">The nervous participants took their seats at the front of the class and looked at the question displayed on their tablets. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.5">The onlookers watched quietly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.110.6">Things like whispering to each other were strictly prohibited until the nominated students had finished submitting their answers.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.111.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.112.1">Time Limit:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.113.1"> 1 minute </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.114.1">15 × 24 × 16 =?</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.115.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.116.1">A multiplication problem. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.116.2">Naturally, there was no choice but to solve it by mental calculation. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.116.3">The problem would be a breeze to do on paper, but much harder to do in your head. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.116.4">Although it seemed pretty easy to me, from the way the nominated students were panicking, it was clear they were struggling to arrive at the answer. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.116.5">The minute passed by in a blink of an eye, and the results showed…one student answered correctly.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.1">Only Ishikura had answered the question correctly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.2">The other two returned to their seats apologetically. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.3">Sakayanagi’s choices in the first round were quite interesting, which made me curious. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.4">Of all the students in Horikita’s class, Ishikura was the best at mathematics. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.5">Even if mental calculation wasn’t exactly the same category, they were related enough. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.6">There was no need for Sakayanagi to take the risk of her opponent getting a correct answer. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.117.7">Even factoring in the seven who had excluded the category, there were several other weak students she could have targeted.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.118.1">It wasn’t impossible that Sakayanagi was simply ignorant of Ishikura’s abilities; Sakayanagi had seen Ishikura tackling math problems in our final exams at the end of our first year. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.118.2">It was difficult to imagine that Sakayanagi would have overlooked that. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.118.3">Was it possible that Sakayanagi decided that a student who was good at performing calculations, like Ishikura, wouldn’t be defended? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.118.4">Maybe she’d chosen Ishikura to test her.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.1">The first turn ended. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.2">A not-too-shabby three points gained right out of the gate. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.3">It was on to turn two, Horikita once again notified the instructor of the five students that she was selecting as the attacker. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.4">Ichinose’s class failed to defend anyone we chose, but two of their students answered correctly, bringing them to a total of six points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.5">One student was protected in Ryuuen’s class and one person answered correctly, bringing their total to three points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.119.6">One student was protected in Sakayanagi’s class, and three students answered correctly, so they now had eight. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.120.1">The gap between the classes was already widening in the second round. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.120.2">Then came time for Horikita’s class’s second round of defending. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.120.3">This time, Sakayanagi changed her choice of category from Calculation to Gastronomy, with a difficulty level of 1. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.120.4">I had no idea what gastronomy was, but I was sure that Horikita knew and had prepared accordingly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.120.5">Horikita decisively notified Sakagami-sensei of which five people she’d chosen for protection.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.121.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.122.1">Successful protections by the defender:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.123.1"> None</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.124.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.125.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.126.1">Kouenji Rokusuke, Hasebe Haruka, Hirata Yousuke, Yukimura Teruhiko, Onodera Kayano</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.127.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.128.1">Unfortunately, all of Horikita’s choices for protection had been in vain. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.128.2">Kouenji’s name was listed among the five nominations as early as the second round. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.128.3">This was a lengthy battle, and even Kouenji was likely to be nominated in at least one round. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.128.4">That in itself was nothing special, but what was important was whether Kouenji could defend himself without help.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.129.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.130.1">“When eating French cuisine, what does it mean when the knife and fork are placed on top of the plate, with the bottoms of the utensils pointing at 4 and 8 o’clock?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.131.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.132.1">It was a simple etiquette question, which I could have answered even before my education at this school. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.132.2">However, Kouenji got it incorrect as he’d left the answer field blank. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.132.3">From behind him, I saw he hadn’t even picked up his stylus. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.132.4">As for the four other students, unfortunately, it looked as though Keisei had mistakenly given the wrong answer; he smacked his desk in frustration immediately after the correct answer was announced. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.132.5">The results were disappointing, but our class still received three points—we were up to six.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.133.1">“Hey, I saw that, Kouenji! </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.133.2">I knew you weren’t gonna take this seriously…!” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.133.3">snapped Sudou.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.134.1">He wasn’t screaming his head off in a rage or anything, but Sudou was angry. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.134.2">His snappy reprimand came across more like he was scolding Kouenji on behalf of the class rather than venting a personal frustration. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.134.3">If Kouenji wasn’t going to even try to solve the problem, it was no wonder Sudou felt frustrated. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.135.1">“No need to blame me. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.135.2">If you are dissatisfied, simply protect me moving forward,” said Kouenji.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.136.1">“Damn it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.136.2">You’re so selfish,” said Sudou.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.137.1">His disapproval was understandable, but until Kouenji got a second question wrong, Sudou didn’t have to worry too much about it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.137.2">The class probably felt a sense of security with Horikita, who was unconcerned despite Kouenji’s refusal to answer any questions. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.137.3">It would’ve been a problem if Horikita didn’t keep her promise and made it her mission to expel Kouenji, but she probably didn’t want to use her precious protection slot without a good reason. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.137.4">Horikita stood confidently; Kouenji glanced over at her for a moment, grinned, and returned to his own seat. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.137.5">Meanwhile, Keisei, who had answered the problem incorrectly simply out of ignorance, came over to Horikita to apologize.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.138.1">“I’m sorry, Horikita. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.138.2">Maybe it was because I was nervous, but the answer just didn’t come to me… I should have known it,” he said.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.139.1">“I’m not that worried about you. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.139.2">If they attack you again in the same category, I’ll defend you when the time comes, okay?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.139.3">said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.140.1">Sakayanagi would not overlook an opening to exploit. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.140.2">That was why Keisei, aware of the danger and solid in his faith Horikita would protect him, nodded meekly to her, to which she responded with a nod of her own. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.141.1">The battle had begun in earnest. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.141.2">Every time a problem was posed, the students would concentrate wholly on their cell phone screens, coming up with their own countermeasures. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.142.1">But while the leaders didn’t have to worry about being nominated, they had less time to rest than anyone else. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.142.2">How would you submit questions to your opponents? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.142.3">How prepared were you to adapt to the moment and change your nominations? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.142.4">Horikita, having little time to so much as speak, hurriedly scrawled notes in her phone or, at times, in an open notebook. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.142.5">Then came Sakayanagi’s attack for the third turn.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.1">Once again, it was the Gastronomy category. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.2">The difficulty level remained the same, at one. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.3">I had thought that Sakayanagi wouldn’t have chosen the same category because the previous question had been an easy one, with three students answering correctly, but apparently our opponent had a plan. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.4">Did Sakayanagi see Kouenji and Keisei’s mistakes as an opportunity to exploit? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.5">They were both powerful classmates. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.6">Horikita had chosen to protect Keisei, just as she had told him she would before, and had then selected her remaining four nominations. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.143.7">However…</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.144.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.145.1">Successful protections by the defender: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.146.1">None</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.147.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.148.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.149.1">Kouenji Rokusuke, Miyamoto Soushi, Ijuuin Wataru, Satou Maya, Azuma Sana</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.150.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.151.1">This was the second time in a row that Sakayanagi had nominated Kouenji, and the other four nominations were completely different. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.151.2">Keisei hadn’t even been targeted.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.152.1">“She read me like a book…” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.1">That was exactly right—attacking and defending was a matter of reading your opponent’s moves. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.2">If you attacked in the same category, it was a given that the defender would take action to prevent an elimination. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.3">There would be no point in targeting Keisei since there was a possibility that he would be protected. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.4">However, the same was also true for Kouenji. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.5">How did Kouenji and Keisei differ in Sakayanagi’s mind? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.6">And how had she been so accurate? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.153.7">One thing was certain: Sakayanagi had gotten a good read on our class’s mind.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.154.1">Kouenji stood up from his seat once again and walked proudly to the front. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.155.1">“Kouenji-kun, I can’t force you to make a promise to the class. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.155.2">However, I think that it would be safer for you to answer correctly,” said Yousuke.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.156.1">Once all the nominated students were seated at the designated desks and it was time to answer the questions, private conversations amongst the audience were strictly prohibited. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.156.2">Yousuke had no other choice but to make his plea while Kouenji walked past. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.156.3">However, Kouenji had no doubts he’d be protected in the following rounds, so once again his answer was blank. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.156.4">His classmates couldn’t contain their emotions at the sight of this, but the other four students answered correctly.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.157.1">The issue of the third turn was simpler: a commonsense problem. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.157.2">If Kouenji had done what he was supposed to, our class would have had the chance to get a perfect score even if he wasn’t offered protection. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.157.3">No one could really feel happy about that. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.157.4">The ranking as of the end of the third turn was as follows: first place, Sakayanagi’s class, eleven points; second place, Horikita’s class, ten points; third place, Ichinose’s class, nine points; last place, Ryuuen’s class, five points.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.1">If Kouenji had done his best, Horikita’s class would have been in the lead with twelve points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.2">As long as Kouenji was only looking out for himself, there was nothing we could do about it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.3">Sakayanagi’s class had taken a solid lead right away since the exam began and firmly held onto first place. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.4">I’m sure Ryuuen had wanted to put a stop to that, but in the first half of this exam, all he could do was watch. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.5">Everything depended on his skill. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.158.6">However, Ryuuen’s class was lagging both on the offense and defense—they were really struggling.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.159.1">It seemed representative of the pure academic ability of the students rather than anticipating each other’s moves or luck. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.159.2">Then came the fourth turn, Horikita’s defense.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.160.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.161.1">Category: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.162.1">Gastronomy</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.163.1">Difficulty Level:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.164.1"> 2</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.165.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.166.1">Sakayanagi had unexpectedly chosen to go with the same category for a third time in a row. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.166.2">This time, however, the difficulty level had been raised to 2. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.166.3">She was spending a point to go in for the kill.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.167.1">“Gastronomy, again? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.167.2">What is Sakayanagi thinking?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.167.3">wondered Sudou.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.168.1">The students were preoccupied with Sakayanagi sticking with the same choice of category rather than the issue of the difficulty level. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.168.2">With Kouenji reaching elimination soon, did Sakayanagi intend to go on the offensive? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.168.3">This was the first time that anyone had increased the difficulty level in any of the classes—it was an experimental attack.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.169.1">“They won’t go after Kouenji again…will they?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.169.2">asked one student.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.170.1">“Do you really not get it? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.170.2">I think they might see this as a chance to get rid of Kouenji-kun,” replied another.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.171.1">Kouenji, after deliberately refusing to answer questions in the Gastronomy category twice in a row, no longer had any way out. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.171.2">Should Horikita protect him now that he was in danger of elimination? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.171.3">Or should we guess that Sakayanagi deliberately wouldn’t go after Kouenji, because he was already close to being eliminated, and thus would be protected? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.171.4">No, Sakayanagi’s choice of category was clearly influenced by her target, with Kouenji dead center.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.172.1">Our situation was different from those of the other classes. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.172.2">It was an issue that went beyond tactics. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.172.3">If Kouenji were to refuse to answer again, he would be the first elimination in our class. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.172.4">Since Horikita had promised to protect him well in advance, she had no other choice but to do so if she wanted to keep her promise. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.172.5">If the opponent came at us and selected Kouenji, we could get a point by choosing to protect him. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.173.1">All eyes were on Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.174.1">Kouenji’s name was not among the five she mentioned.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.175.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.176.1">Successful protections by the defender: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.177.1">Shinohara Satsuki, Sudou Ken</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.178.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.179.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.180.1">Kouenji Rokusuke, Sotomura Hideo, Miyake Akito</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.181.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.182.1">Our classmates, who had watched in relative silence up until this point, were visibly bewildered. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.183.1">“H-Horikita-san?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.183.2">said Yousuke.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.184.1">Yousuke was most surprised of all. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.184.2">He’d never doubted her for a moment, and now he leaped to his feet.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.185.1">“You sure about this, Suzune? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.185.2">I mean, if Kouenji messes this one up, he’s eliminated, right?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.185.3">said Sudou.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.186.1">Sudou was similarly shocked. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.186.2">However, Horikita just quietly stared ahead. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.186.3">The only person whose facial expression didn’t change a bit at this turn of events was Kouenji—the very person who should have absolutely been panicking.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.187.1">“Heh heh heh</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman__And__Italic"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.188.1">.</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.189.1"> You’re really giving it to me, eh, Horikita Girl?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.189.2">said Kouenji.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.190.1">To people who didn’t think about the matter too deeply, it looked like Horikita had abandoned Kouenji, that she was someone who went back on her promises. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.190.2">It was a betrayal. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.190.3">I couldn’t say throwing away the trust of the class was the best course of action, considering everything that had happened since the Unanimous Special Exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.190.4">Kouenji said nothing further and sat down at the front like the other students.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.1">The problem that they were presented with was certainly more difficult than the previous two. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.2">I caught glimpses of students exchanging glances and cocking their heads to the side in confusion. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.3">I didn’t know how much Kouenji knew about gastronomy, but there was an uneasy feeling in the air. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.4">The young man who hadn’t even picked up the stylus pen the previous two times had finally made a move. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.5">Watching him, I got the impression that his hands moved swiftly, not even a hint of hesitation. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.191.6">But would his answer reflect that confidence?</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.192.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.193.1">Students who answered correctly: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.194.1">Kouenji Rokusuke</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.195.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.196.1">Kouenji’s name appeared in the list of students who gave correct answers for the first time. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.196.2">He’d managed to avoid elimination.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.197.1">“Wow, so someone was worried after all, huh?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.197.2">hollered Sudou. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.197.3">He was teasing, but there was also a note of relief in his voice. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.197.4">Even those who disliked Kouenji didn’t want him to be eliminated. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.198.1">“You have an active imagination,” said Kouenji.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.199.1">I didn’t know what Kouenji was thinking, but his reaction was sensible. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.199.2">One more wrong answer and he would’ve been a candidate for expulsion. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.199.3">What concerned me was Horikita. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.199.4">Even if Horikita assumed that Kouenji would’ve taken the problem seriously if he were backed into a corner, had Kouenji gotten it wrong, things would’ve been bad.</span></span></p>
