<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.200.1">Horikita needed to defend him, even if she were confident that he could’ve answered correctly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.200.2">The class was clearly anxious, but not even Yousuke could make sense of it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.200.3">The only exception was, of course, the young man in question. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.200.4">Kouenji walked past me, to my side, and stood in front of Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.201.1">“If you have an explanation, I’d like to hear it,” muttered Kouenji.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.202.1">“Explanation? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.202.2">For what?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.202.3">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.203.1">“Oh, it’s like that, is it?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.203.2">After seeing the steel in Horikita’s eyes, Kouenji smiled.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.204.1">“You weren’t eliminated. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.204.2">You shouldn’t be worried about being expelled, right?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.204.3">said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.205.1">“I would have been eliminated had I not answered correctly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.205.2">So what are you playing at?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.206.1">“But you got it correct,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.207.1">“Heh heh heh. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.207.2">Yes, you’re right, I did. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.207.3">Excuse me. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.207.4">It appears as though I’ve hastily jumped to a conclusion.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.208.1">“If we’ve cleared up your misunderstanding, could you please return to your seat? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.208.2">You’re blocking the monitor,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.209.1">Their conversation seemed to imply that Horikita had abandoned Kouenji, and it did nothing to quell everyone’s bewilderment, apart from perhaps Kouenji himself. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.209.2">I could have done something to reassure the students by explaining Horikita’s thinking and the usefulness of not protecting Kouenji, but I remained a neutral observer. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.210.1">Horikita chose not to reassure anyone; she had other aims. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.210.2">In fact, she didn’t even bat an eye at their confusion. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.210.3">She didn’t nominate Kouenji as a candidate for protection during Sakayanagi’s attack on the fifth turn, either. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.210.4">However, Kouenji’s name also disappeared from the list of targets to be attacked.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.211.1">From the perspective of the other students, Kouenji would have been a good target to choose, as they would have seen this as their chance to eliminate him. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.211.2">After all, he wasn’t being protected. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.211.3">However, Sakayanagi chose other targets. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.211.4">Kouenji had been the only person to correctly answer the problem in the gastronomy category with a difficulty level of 2, so it was possible that Sakayanagi had recognized Kouenji was too tough to pick off and chose another tactic. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.211.5">Many of the students here likely had this misunderstanding of the situation.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.212.1">Kouenji had answered the question using his own knowledge. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.212.2">This meant that Sakayanagi could no longer expect he’d make mistakes, which in turn meant that Horikita took a risky gamble that could have cost her the trust of her allies. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.212.3">This time, however, Kouenji’s name was not on the list of candidates. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.213.1">Horikita’s face clouded over upon seeing this. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.213.2">“I guess our opponent won’t fall for it so easily…” she said.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.1">Because I was seated in close proximity to Horikita, I overheard her muttering to herself. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.2">During the first half of the battle, the turns flew by, and I enjoyed the action. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.3">The number of students who made mistakes steadily increased as time went by, and finally, at the end of the seventh turn, Ishizaki from Ryuuen’s class went down as the first student to be eliminated. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.4">In the following turn, turn eight, Sotomura and Ijuuin from Horikita’s class were eliminated simultaneously, while Isoyama and Yano from Ryuuen’s class were the next to go in that same turn. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.5">Then it was Kamuro from Sakayanagi’s class. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.214.6">By the end of turn ten, the first half, Hondou from Horikita’s class, Morofuji from Ryuuen’s class, and Yamamura from Sakayanagi’s class had been eliminated.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.215.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.216.1">HALFTIME RESULTS</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em; text-transform: uppercase;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.217.1">1ST PLACE</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.218.1"> – Sakayanagi, Class A – 29 Points – Eliminations: Kamuro, Yamamura</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em; text-transform: uppercase;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.219.1">2ND PLACE</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.220.1"> – Horikita, Class B – 28 Points – Eliminations: Sotomura, Ijuuin, Hondou</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em; text-transform: uppercase;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.221.1">3RD PLACE</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.222.1"> – Ichinose, Class C – 24 Points – Eliminations: None</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em; text-transform: uppercase;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.223.1">4TH PLACE</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.224.1"> – Ryuuen, Class D – 19 Points – Eliminations: Ishizaki, Isoyama, Yano, Morofuji</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.225.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.226.1">Nine eliminations in all. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.226.2">That might’ve sounded like a lot, but it would get even more brutal in the second half. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.226.3">A number of students had eaten up two of their three allotted mistakes. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.226.4">Ichinose’s class was the only one that still had zero eliminations. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.226.5">At first glance, that appeared to be a fine play on Ichinose’s part, but appearances could be deceiving.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.227.1">“Your strategy worked well, Horikita-san,” said Yousuke, coming over to congratulate her.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.228.1">“I knew that her stance wouldn’t change, even on this special exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.228.2">I’ve managed to box her in,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.229.1">I wondered how many of our classmates had noticed what was happening as Horikita unleashed her strategy against Ichinose. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.229.2">The reason the number of eliminations in her class remained at zero was because Horikita intentionally kept the number of students within range of being eliminated to five. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.229.3">Ichinose would absolutely protect her classmates. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.229.4">Knowing that, Horikita had distributed her attack nominations to avoid creating a sixth person in range of elimination.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.230.1">Ichinose, on the other hand, seemed to have continued to protect those five students in danger of elimination, even though she knew Horikita would spread out her attacks. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.230.2">After those five students had gotten to that point, Ichinose hadn’t gotten even one successful protection because she was protecting them instead. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.230.3">She didn’t want to risk leaving the five students at risk of expulsion, after all. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.231.1">“She really doesn’t back down. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.231.2">It was a ridiculous long-term strategy. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.231.3">Even if she could defend them in the first half, it’s going to get much, much more difficult later,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.232.1">Illustrating her point was the fact that Ichinose’s class had the highest number of students who had gotten one question wrong out of the four classes.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.233.1">“In the second half, Ichinose-san will have to defend her classmates from Ryuuen-kun. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.233.2">I’m sure that will be painful,” added Yousuke.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.234.1">“There is the option of abandoning protecting those five in the second half of the exam, she could just do that, but…” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.235.1">Even so, this was Ryuuen. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.235.2">He wasn’t strategic either.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.236.1">“Anyway, it’s down to us. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.236.2">We have a good chance to eke out a one-point win,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.237.1">Sakayanagi’s class had pulled far into the lead early on, but Horikita was nipping at her heels. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.237.2">By herself, Horikita seemed a half step behind when it came to anticipating Sakayanagi’s moves, but her classmates were skillfully making up the gap.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.238.1">“Regarding the rate of questions answered correctly, putting aside cases of successful protections, it seems that the school expects that approximately half of the students will be able to answer each problem. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.238.2">When the difficulty level increases by one, the rate of correct answers is 20 percent, and when it’s at difficulty level 3, it drops to about 10 percent or so,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.239.1">At a difficulty level of 3, we couldn’t reliably expect anyone to get a correct answer, but that option wasn’t used too often because it cost two points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.239.2">If the opponent defended from it via protection, that would result in a big loss. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.239.3">I imagined it wouldn’t be used too frequently even in the latter half of the exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.239.4">The battle for the top spot was the most interesting part, yes, but the struggle to stay out of final place was also heating up. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.239.5">Ryuuen’s class was in a particularly tight spot.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.240.1">Assuming that the trends we’d seen continued, and assuming that more people would be eliminated, the line to cross for first place would be somewhere between fifty and fifty-five points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.240.2">In the second half alone, Ryuuen would need to gain at least thirty points just to survive. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.240.3">Generally, students with high academic ability tended to be more difficult to target; however, they were difficult to protect too. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.240.4">We saw a smattering of students who got something wrong in unexpected categories. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.241.1">It also seemed like questions in areas not related to academics, like subcultures and gastronomy, were often easier than those related to academics, even when they were technically set at the same difficulty level. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.241.2">Incidentally, I got a question wrong once myself in an academic category. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.241.3">The question was “What is the animal that was bred in a zoo and became a worldwide sensation because of how cute it looked when it stood up on its hind legs?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.241.4">from the News category.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.242.1">I had no idea what animal it was, so I just randomly wrote “dog” as my answer, which prompted an icy look from Horikita. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.243.1">The correct answer was red panda.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.244.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P__STAR__STAR__STAR__page_break"><span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.245.1">7.1</span></span></p>

<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 2.40em; letter-spacing: 0.60mm;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.247.1">W</span></span><span style=" font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.248.1">HEN IT CAME TIME </span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.249.1">for our lunch break, I told Horikita to give me a few minutes of her time and brought her out into the hallway.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.250.1">“It was a red panda, okay?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.250.2">snapped Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.251.1">I never imagined that she would take me to task for something like that right away. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.251.2">“That’s not what this is about. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.251.3">I’m curious about a few things,” I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.252.1">“It was a joke. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.252.2">What is it you need? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.252.3">Advice?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.252.4">she asked.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.253.1">“Nothing worth calling advice, really, but did you notice how the nominated names are displayed?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.253.2">I asked.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.254.1">“No… To be honest, I haven’t been paying any attention to the order. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.254.2">It wasn’t in alphabetical order or in order of boys first and then girls afterward, was it?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.254.3">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.255.1">“I can’t say for certain what the order is without hearing what the other classes said when they were attacking, but there was no systematic rule on the school’s part at all when you had nominated your five, Horikita. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.255.2">That means they were likely shown in the exact order in which the leader said them.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.256.1">“I see. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.256.2">You might be right about that. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.256.3">So?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.256.4">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.257.1">“What got my attention were Sakayanagi’s nominations from turns two through four in the first half of the exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.257.2">Kouenji was targeted three times in a row, and in every case, he was the first one nominated,” I explained.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.258.1">“So she decided to go after Kouenji-kun by the second turn, and she continued to pick him first until he answered a question correctly…? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.258.2">If I remember right, Yukimura-kun got the question wrong on the second turn too, didn’t he?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.258.3">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.259.1">“Yes, he did. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.259.2">Kouenji would be a threat as well if you looked at him in terms of his abilities alone, but overall, Keisei should be seen as more of a problem. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.259.3">Yet, Sakayanagi didn’t even nominate Keisei on the third turn, after he had made a mistake,” I answered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.260.1">“Don’t you think that she’s simply not so good at anticipating my moves? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.260.2">She might’ve decided that I’d protect Yukimura-kun because I thought he was important, right?” </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.1">“Yes, it’s true that in Keisei’s case, she might have removed him from her list of targets for that reason, but that doesn’t explain what happened with Kouenji. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.2">During the first half of the exam, he got the question wrong twice in a row, on turns two and three, and then answered correctly on turn four. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.3">His name didn’t show up even once after that point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.4">If he were being defended via protection, that would make sense, but he got the question right on his own on turn four. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.5">Which means that we had never protected him once. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.261.6">Our opponents would’ve known that, too,” I answered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.262.1">“Sakayanagi-san initially had her sights set only on Kouenji-kun. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.262.2">She had gotten him in range of elimination after he had answered incorrectly twice, but she simply stopped attacking once he had gotten one question correct. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.262.3">Very unusual,” mused Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.263.1">It would be better to eliminate as many students as possible. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.263.2">If there was a good chance that he was not going to be protected, then Sakayanagi should have kept attacking.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.264.1">“Is she perhaps wary of his knowledge?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.264.2">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.265.1">“If so, she wouldn’t have attacked Kouenji from the very beginning. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.265.2">It doesn’t explain why she nominated him three times in a row,” I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.266.1">“…Is Sakayanagi-san aware of the promise that I made to Kouenji-kun?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.266.2">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.267.1">“I think that’s a safe bet. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.267.2">With that promise in hand, Kouenji wouldn’t take this exam seriously. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.267.3">She must have calculated that you wouldn’t have defended him until he got two questions wrong,” I replied.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.268.1">The chances that Kouenji would have taken the questions seriously from the start or that Horikita would have defended him right away weren’t zero. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.268.2">Sakayanagi would have changed strategies in that case.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.269.1">“Why didn’t she target him from turn five on, then? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.269.2">I had chosen not to protect him then, remember?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.269.3">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.270.1">“She didn’t precisely because you made that choice. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.270.2">If her intention was to make you get rid of one of your protection slots by using it on Kouenji, and that plan was no longer viable, she must have felt like it was a waste of time—disadvantageous to her, in fact,” I reasoned.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.271.1">“Even though we lose a point if someone is eliminated?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.271.2">asked Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.272.1">“Yeah. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.272.2">You said as much yourself before the exam. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.272.3">You said that you had a plan in place to minimize the damage in the event that our class came in last place. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.272.4">That option was to let Kouenji be eliminated, wasn’t it?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.273.1">“…So you knew.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.274.1">“Your promise with Kouenji was that you wouldn’t ‘expel’ him. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.274.2">You’d let him be ‘free.’ </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.274.3">In this special exam, you haven’t asked anything of Kouenji, so it goes without saying that you’ve kept your promise to give him his freedom. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.274.4">As for the other part, about you not expelling him…hypothetically, if Kouenji were to be the only one to be eliminated, he wouldn’t be expelled if he uses his Protect Point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.274.5">Problem solved,” I answered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.275.1">Kouenji had won that Protect Point on his own, by winning in the Uninhabited Island Exam, granting him the right to nullify an expulsion.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.276.1">“That’s right. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.276.2">I hadn’t promised that I would protect Kouenji-kun’s Protect Point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.276.3">As long as he isn’t expelled, I’ve fulfilled my promise. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.276.4">He would have no right whatsoever to resent me over it,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.277.1">Even though we would lose one point for an elimination, all Horikita would have to do is strip away Kouenji’s Protect Point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.277.2">There would be no risk of anyone getting expelled if we were in last place in that scenario.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.278.1">“I’m sure I worried everyone, though,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.279.1">“If you did explain, Kouenji would know what you’re trying to do, right?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.279.2">I answered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.280.1">“Yes. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.280.2">But he seemed to catch on right away. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.280.3">Personally, it would have been easier for me in the second half of the exam if he had been eliminated early.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.281.1">That was why Kouenji had answered correctly on his own—it would have annoyed him to have his Protect Point stripped away.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.282.1">“It’s reasonable to assume that, because of Sakayanagi’s personality, she decided that she didn’t want to erase the pressure of possible expulsion from our class, Horikita.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.283.1">“Her personality comes through in every one of her actions. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.283.2">But why didn’t Kouenji-kun give correct answers right from the start?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.284.1">“Not even I know the answer to that question. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.284.2">Maybe he just decided he could hold out until the third question. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.284.3">Anyway, there may be students in the class who are leaking information.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.285.1">That was invaluable to keep in mind, not just for the special exam but for after as well.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.286.1">“Thank you. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.286.2">I’ll be sure to keep that in mind,” said Horikita.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.287.1">“That’s all I needed to say. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.287.2">What are you going to do for lunch?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.288.1">“I didn’t have the time to make one, so I think I’ll go to the cafeteria today. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.288.2">What about you?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.289.1">“I think I’ll do the same. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.289.2">Kei will probably be staring at her phone the entire time,” I replied, turning toward the classroom as I said that. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.290.1">Horikita nodded in apparent understanding. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.290.2">Kei had gotten through the first half of the exam unscathed, as she hadn’t been nominated by Sakayanagi even once. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.290.3">However, I couldn’t say that came as a relief to her. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.290.4">In as few as three questions, she could join the ranks of the eliminated. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.290.5">She probably wanted to drill as much knowledge as possible into her head, even if it was just a stopgap measure.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.291.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P__STAR__STAR__STAR__page_break"><span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.292.1">7.2</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.293.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 2.40em; letter-spacing: 0.60mm;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.294.1">T</span></span><span style=" font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.295.1">HE SURVIVAL AND ELIMINATION</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.296.1"> Special Exam had begun, and was progressing slowly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.296.2">Ryuuen weathered his first attack from Ichinose’s class, but with only one out of five protections being successful and no students answering correctly, no one felt especially good about it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.296.3">That was understandable: academics were a weak point in Ryuuen’s class.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.297.1">Even putting aside the students who had excluded Economics, the category that Ichinose had chosen, nearly half of the students in Ryuuen’s class felt anxious about the category. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.297.2">On the other hand, Ichinose’s class, which had been attacked by Horikita’s class, had scored a total of four points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.297.3">A three-point gap had already opened by the end of the first turn, and a gloomy feeling hung over the class. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.297.4">However, that wasn’t because they didn’t gain points.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.298.1">“Okay then! </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.298.2">As the attacker, who do you plan to nominate from Class A?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.298.3">said Hoshinomiya, cheerfully guiding the leader.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.299.1">But Ryuuen didn’t budge. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.299.2">He stared at his cell phone in calm silence.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.300.1">“Hello? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.300.2">Can you hear me?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.300.3">asked Hoshinomiya.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.301.1">She called out again from the podium just to be sure, but Ryuuen remained still. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.301.2">As was understood from the explanation of the rules, the nomination time was steadily ticking away, one second at a time. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.301.3">Though Hoshinomiya figured that Ryuuen would have surely decided on who they would attack on the first turn and belt it out, Ryuuen still hadn’t moved in the first minute. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.301.4">Normally, you would’ve expected to hear the voices of classmates piping up with people asking, “Are you okay?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.301.5">but no one did. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.1">Even if the majority of people in this class wanted to say something, they couldn’t. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.2">Now, in battle, Ryuuen exuded an even more overwhelming and intimidating air than ever before. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.3">It was rare for Hoshinomiya to see Ryuuen’s class so tense. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.4">Ryuuen’s classmates, however, were a little more used to it—this was an everyday occurrence. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.5">It was something they saw all the time. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.302.6">It would’ve been quicker if Kaneda, the general adviser, made a move on Ryuuen’s behalf, but since he tended to wait for instructions from Ryuuen, no one was bothered by his silence.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.303.1">At times like this, uncertain classmates tended to turn their eyes to Katsuragi. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.303.2">Although he was a transfer from another class, the students considered him Ryuuen’s chief strategist. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.303.3">In addition to Katsuragi’s overall strengths in OAA, he didn’t cower before Ryuuen’s surly temper. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.303.4">If all it took were defiance, Ibuki would have qualified, but in Katsuragi’s case, he had reasons for acting the way he did.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.304.1">Katsuragi, the person the other students would usually turn to, didn’t move a muscle. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.304.2">With eyes closed and arms folded across his chest, he allowed time to pass while he was on the attacking side. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.304.3">Had he figured that calling out to Ryuuen in this situation wouldn’t change anything and thus gave up on even trying? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.304.4">Or was he merely confident? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.304.5">In either case, the students could only watch in silence.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.305.1">“Hey, listen, it’s still only the first turn, okay? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.305.2">Three points isn’t that big of a difference, so you shouldn’t get too worked up about it,” Hoshinomiya said, offering them encouragement.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.306.1">They just hadn’t pulled ahead on the first attack, that was all. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.306.2">It might have seemed a little biased, but she couldn’t remain silent in the face of the students before her and their anxieties. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.306.3">That was her official stance, anyway.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.307.1">In reality, if Sakayanagi’s class pulled too far ahead, it’d be hard for Ichinose’s class, the class that she was in charge of, to win. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.307.2">If Sakayanagi’s class continued to win high scores time and time again with their cowardly strategies, her own class would have no chance of winning. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.307.3">It was a calculated move on her part to say something, but as Hoshinomiya watched the class continue in silence, she realized that she had made an error in judgment. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.307.4">Even though many students questioned Ryuuen’s lack of action, few actually felt anxious about it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.307.5">Normally, silence often brought about negative consequences, but in this class, it was a unique strength they had cultivated.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.308.1">Hoshinomiya accepted the unusual situation: with nearly two minutes having passed now and still no attempt to so much as utter the names of their nominees. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman__And__Italic"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.309.1">That silence holds secrets.</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.310.1"> Hoshinomiya started thinking that way for the first time. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.310.2">In that brief moment, what fantastic strategy would they come up with? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.310.3">An ideal set of nominations against Sakayanagi’s class, where they would fail to protect anyone and get many of the questions wrong? </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.311.1">When the countdown neared thirty seconds remaining, Ryuuen gave Hoshinomiya the five names.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.312.1">“O-oh, w-w-wait a minute! </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.312.2">Let me enter them,” said Hoshinomiya, quickly tapping the names in.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.313.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.314.1">Category:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.315.1"> Everyday Life</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.316.1">Difficulty Level: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.317.1">1</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.318.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.319.1">Students nominated by the attacker:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.320.1"> Kitou Hayato, Kamuro Masumi, Hashimoto Masayoshi, Machida Kouji, Yamamura Miki</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.321.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.322.1">Hoshinomiya hurriedly finished inputting the information but was astonished by the choices. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.322.2">Ryuuen had picked students who were close to Sakayanagi. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.323.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.324.1">Successful protections by the defender: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.325.1">Machida Kouji</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.326.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.327.1">In the end, after all that consideration, one person had been protected. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.327.2">The problem was what came after.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.328.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.329.1">Students who answered correctly: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.330.1">Kitou Hayato, Kamuro Masumi, Hashimoto Masayoshi</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.331.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.1">Three of the four remaining students had answered correctly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.2">That meant Ryuuen’s class had given the opposing class four points while he had only gotten one. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.3">A bad start. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.4">Not much could be expected from them. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.5">Hoshinomiya had been impressed as she looked at the students, who seemed surprisingly calm, but now, inwardly, she had taken that feeling back. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.332.6">She realized that they weren’t as good as she thought after all, and it was unlikely that they’d be able to crush Sakayanagi’s class.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.333.1">From that point onward, you couldn’t say that Ryuuen’s strategy had been skillful, even if you were feeling especially generous. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.333.2">Most of the people he had nominated had been the same people he’d nominated in the first turn. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.333.3">Although he occasionally switched it up, possibly as a diversion, he continued to nominate Kitou, Kamuro, Hashimoto, Yamamura, Machida, Sanada, Satonaka, and Matoba once every two turns or so. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.333.4">Sakayanagi started to predict his pattern and adjusted protections accordingly.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.334.1">Even so, Ryuuen didn’t drastically change his nominations. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.334.2">He just spent more turns at the bottom of the rankings. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.334.3">On the fifth turn, the midpoint of the first half of the exam, Hoshinomiya noticed something inexplicable. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.334.4">Even though some students had already answered two questions incorrectly and they ought to be panicking…</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.335.1">“They’re all so calm,” she muttered.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.336.1">They couldn’t get themselves out of last place because they had a weak defense. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.336.2">Since they clearly had a lower percentage of correct answers to problems compared to the other classes, they weren’t earning points where they should have been. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.336.3">However, even in this situation, few students were feeling nervous. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.336.4">Hoshinomiya strolled around the classroom, pretending to monitor them, and casually peaked at each student’s phone.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.337.1">They weren’t just playing around, but rather, they were browsing various websites and checking out videos to study the categories they were weak in. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.337.2">Was it simply that they were too nervous to speak up? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.337.3">Or maybe because they were under Ryuuen’s control? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.337.4">That thought had occurred to Hoshinomiya too, but…</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.338.1">“Kaneda-kun, it doesn’t appear that you’re doing anything. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.338.2">I assume you are fully prepared, yes?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.338.3">asked Hoshinomiya, suddenly reaching out to Kaneda. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.338.4">He wasn’t even touching his phone while the rest of the students were silently preparing.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.339.1">“I always make an effort to study, and I don’t like cramming at the last minute. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.339.2">It isn’t good to disrupt routine,” announced Kaneda, pushing up his glasses, flashing a fearless grin.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.340.1">“I-I see. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.340.2">Smart kids certainly are built different,” replied Hoshinomiya.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.341.1">Hoshinomiya, slightly taken aback by Kaneda’s response—which just fell short of being “</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman__And__Italic"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.342.1">Why even bother asking?”</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.343.1">—had lost Kaneda’s interest. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.343.2">Students like Ishizaki even had the audacity to doze off while they were waiting for their turn. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.343.3">Ishizaki had already gotten two questions wrong and seemed like he had accepted his fate.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.344.1">“What on earth is going on in this class…?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.345.1">She felt a little uncomfortable, but still, she performed her duties as teacher turn after turn.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.346.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P__STAR__STAR__STAR__page_break"><span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.347.1">7.3</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.348.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 2.40em; letter-spacing: 0.60mm;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.349.1">I</span></span><span style=" font-size: 0.80em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.350.1">MMEDIATELY AFTER SAKAYANAGI </span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.351.1">had informed the teacher about which five targets she had nominated in her second attack against Horikita’s class, Hashimoto stood and walked over to where Sakayanagi was seated. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.351.2">In place of his usual grin was a stern expression. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.351.3">It was bizarrely conspicuous behavior considering everyone other than Hashimoto remained in their seats.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.352.1">“Whatever is the matter, Hashimoto-kun?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.352.2">asked Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.353.1">“It’s just, I know I gave you information last night, just in case. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.353.2">I’m getting the feeling that you don’t intend to make use of it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.353.3">Am I right?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.354.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.355.1">Category:</span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.356.1"> Gastronomy</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.357.1">Difficulty Level: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.358.1">1</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.359.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.360.1">Students nominated by the attacker: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.361.1">Kouenji Rokusuke, Hasebe Haruka, Hirata Yousuke, Yukimura Teruhiko, Onodera Kayano</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.362.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.363.1">Hashimoto, pointing at the monitor behind him with his thumb, indicated he was unhappy with her choices.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.364.1">“Is that how it looks to you?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.364.2">asked Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.365.1">“Yeah, it does,” replied Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.366.1">“While it is certainly true that your phone call last night was overly meddlesome, the information you gave me was only that—information. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.366.2">I will not ignore it, of course. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.366.3">It has been firmly etched into my hippocampus,” said Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.367.1">“Okay. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.367.2">Then, in that case…why did you target Kouenji?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.367.3">asked Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.368.1">“You said that Kouenji-kun was one of the targets in Class B that we ought to avoid the most, isn’t that right?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.368.2">replied Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.369.1">“He has that promise with Horikita. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.369.2">He’s a likely candidate for protection, so if you go after him, he’ll probably be defended, and you’ll just be handing them points. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.369.3">I was hoping you could use at least </span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman__And__Italic"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.370.1">some</span></span><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.371.1"> of the information I gave you.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.372.1">Hashimoto couldn’t stand being trampled underfoot so quickly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.372.2">Kitou, sensing that the situation had grown grim, slowly pulled out his chair.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.1">“Don’t worry, Kitou-kun. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.2">Hashimoto-kun just happens to have a dry sense of humor,” said Sakayanagi. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.3">She chuckled and turned back to Hashimoto. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.4">“I’m sure there is an arrangement between Horikita-san and Kouenji-kun. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.5">However, that arrangement has two stipulations. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.6">First, that she would simply not expel him, and second, that she would allow him his freedom. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.7">Yes?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.373.8">replied Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.374.1">“Yeah…” replied Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.375.1">“There is no advantage to be gained for her in wasting one of her precious protection slots to continuously defend him. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.375.2">At the very least, she would wait and see what happens until he has been targeted once and has answered a question incorrectly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.375.3">She would need to do at least that much in order to win. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.375.4">Wouldn’t you agree?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.375.5">asked Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.376.1">“But Horikita’s honest. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.376.2">If she won’t protect him, her class will be upset,” said Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.377.1">“If they’re going to be upset about something as trivial as that, she would sooner let them be upset. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.377.2">While the actual fulfillment of her promise is important, if she wasted her protection slots on Kouenji-kun, she’d be unfit to lead.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.378.1">While Sakayanagi was explaining this, Horikita’s class had decided on which five students they would protect. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.378.2">The monitor refreshed. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.378.3">It now showed that none of Horikita’s protections had been successful, and the five students that Sakayanagi had nominated would be tasked with solving a problem.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.379.1">“What do you think? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.379.2">As expected, she’s left Kouenji-kun undefended,” said Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.380.1">Hashimoto had to let the matter drop.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.381.1">“…Okay, sure. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.381.2">But what’s the point in forcing ourselves to get a point from Kouenji, anyway? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.381.3">The dude’s weirdly smart. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.381.4">He’s far more likely to get correct answers compared to some nobody, right?”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.382.1">“Do you think so? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.382.2">He’s a capricious fellow, undeniably. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.382.3">He’s such a free spirit that he even defies Horikita-san. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.382.4">He’s under no obligation to take these questions seriously. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.382.5">He might even answer them incorrectly on purpose,” said Sakayanagi.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.383.1">Sakayanagi was unwavering in her conviction as she spoke. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.383.2">It was almost like she was claiming she could read the future. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.383.3">Hashimoto waited for the monitor to refresh, wondering if she had that kind of power. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.383.4">The results showed that Kouenji did indeed answer incorrectly, just as Sakayanagi had predicted. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.383.5">He was one step closer to elimination. </span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.384.1">“Well, you took a risk, but you got a point. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.384.2">Brilliantly done, Princess,” said Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.1">Hashimoto felt momentarily relieved, but that relief was dashed away in the following turn. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.2">As soon as their attack phase began, Sakayanagi, without even a moment’s hesitation, went ahead and announced Kouenji’s name as the first of her nominations. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.3">In the same category, to boot. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.4">It was as though she were announcing to everyone that she was targeting him. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.5">Even Sakayanagi’s other classmates stirred. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.385.6">Kamuro raised an objection.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.386.1">“Hold up, what are you doing? </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.386.2">You picked the same category, so it’s obvious that they’re going to protect Kouenji,” said Kamuro.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.387.1">“You’re not saying he won’t be protected this time either, are you?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.387.2">asked Hashimoto.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.388.1">“Yes, I would bet on it. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.388.2">That’s why I nominated him.”</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.389.1">It was an absurd prediction on Sakayanagi’s part. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.389.2">He peered at the monitor from his seat and waited for the results.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.390.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span style=" font-weight: bold; font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.391.1">Successful protections by the defender: </span></span><span style=" font-size: 1.00em;"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.392.1">None</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.393.1">&#160;</span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.394.1">“Wait, seriously…?! </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.394.2">What is Horikita thinking?” </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.394.3">Hashimoto blurted out.</span></span></p>
<p class="P_Prose_Formatting"><span class="C_Current__And__Times_New_Roman"><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.395.1">And once again, Kouenji had answered the question incorrectly. </span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" class="koboSpan" id="kobo.395.2">That kind of mistake was unthinkable from him.</span></span></p>
